. Then we have

\displaystyle|\not{\nabla}U|_{p,S}+r^{-1}|U|_{p,S}\lesssim|f|_{p,S}.

Proof.

See Corollary 2.3.1.1 in
[
5
]
.
∎

Proposition 4.13
.

Let
F
be a tensor field on
S
. Then, we have

|r^{\frac{1}{2}}F|_{\infty,S}\lesssim|F|_{4,S}+|r\not{\nabla}F|_{4,S}.

Proof.

See Lemma 4.1.3 in
[
15
]
.
∎

4.5
Elliptic estimates in
3D
–geometry

Throughout Section
4.5
, we assume that
t\geq 0
is a fixed constant and we denote
\Sigma:=\Sigma_{t}
the maximal hypersurface constructed in Section
2.1.1
.

Definition 4.14
.

For a tensor field
h
on
\Sigma
, we define its
L^{p}
–norm as follows:

\|h\|_{p,\Sigma}:=\left(\int_{\Sigma}|h|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(4.4)

Proposition 4.15

(Hardy)
.

We have for any tensor field
F
on
\Sigma

\displaystyle\|r^{-1}F\|_{2,\Sigma}\leq c_{H}\|\nabla F\|_{2,\Sigma},

where

c_{H}=2+O(\varepsilon).

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\sdiv N=g^{NN}g(D_{N}N,N)+g^{AB}g(D_{A}N,e_{B})=\not{g}^{AB}\theta_{AB}=\tr\theta=\frac{2}{r}+\Gamma_{b},

(4.5)

which implies from
r\Gamma_{b}=O(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\sdiv\left(\frac{N}{r}\right)=\frac{\sdiv N}{r}-\frac{N(r)}{r^{2}}=\frac{1}{r^{2}}+r^{-1}\Gamma_{b}=\frac{1+O(\varepsilon)}{r^{2}}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle(1+O(\varepsilon))\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|F|^{2}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\sdiv\left(\frac{N}{r}\right)|F|^{2}=-\int_{\Sigma}\frac{2}{r}F\cdot\nabla_{N}F\leq 2\left(\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|F|^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Sigma}|\nabla_{N}F|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which implies

\displaystyle\|r^{-1}F\|_{2,\Sigma}\leq(2+O(\varepsilon))\|\nabla F\|_{2,\Sigma}.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
4.15
.
∎

Proposition 4.16

(Sobolev)
.

Let
F
be a tensor field, tangent to
S
at every point. We have the following inequalities:

\displaystyle\|F\|_{6,\Sigma}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla F\|_{2,\Sigma},

\displaystyle\sup_{S\subseteq\Sigma}|F|_{4,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla F\|_{2,\Sigma},

\displaystyle\sup_{S\subseteq\Sigma}|r^{\frac{1}{2}}{\langle u\rangle}^{\frac{1}{2}}F|_{4,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|F\|_{2,\Sigma}+\|r\not{\nabla}F\|_{2,\Sigma}+\|{\langle u\rangle}\not{\nabla}_{N}F\|_{2,\Sigma}.

Proof.

See Corollaries 3.2.1.1 and 3.2.1.2 in
[
5
]
.
∎

Proposition 4.17

(Gagliardo-Nirenberg)
.

Given
F
a tensor field on
\Sigma
, we have the following inequality:

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\infty,\Sigma}\lesssim\|F\|_{6,\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla F\|_{6,\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

See Page 308 in
[
5
]
.
∎

Proposition 4.18
.

Let
\xi
be a
1
–form on
\Sigma
satisfying

\displaystyle\sdiv\xi

\displaystyle=D(\xi),

\displaystyle\curl\xi

\displaystyle=A(\xi).

Then, the following integral identity holds:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|\nabla\xi|^{2}=\int_{\Sigma}|A(\xi)|^{2}+|D(\xi)|^{2}-\sRic^{ij}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}.

Proof.

See Lemma 4.4.1 in
[
5
]
.
∎

Proposition 4.19
.

Let
U
be a
2
–traceless symmetric tensor on
\Sigma
satisfying

\displaystyle\sdiv U

\displaystyle=D(U),

\displaystyle\curl U

\displaystyle=A(U).

Then, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|\nabla U|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\Sigma}|A(U)|^{2}+|D(U)|^{2}+|\sRic||U|^{2},

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|\nabla^{2}U|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\Sigma}|\nabla A(U)|^{2}+|\nabla D(U)|^{2}+|\sRic||\nabla U|^{2}+|\sRic|^{2}|U|^{2}.

Proof.

See Propositions 4.4.1 and 4.4.2 in
[
5
]
.
∎

Proposition 4.20
.

Let
\phi
be a scalar function on
\Sigma
satisfying

\displaystyle\Delta\phi=f.

Then, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|\nabla\phi|^{2}+r^{2}|\nabla^{2}\phi|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\Sigma}r^{2}|f|^{2},

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}r^{4}|\not{\nabla}^{3}\phi|^{2}+r^{4}|\not{\nabla}^{2}\nabla_{N}\phi|^{2}+r^{4}|\not{\nabla}\nabla_{N}^{2}\phi|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\Sigma}r^{2}|f|^{2}+r^{4}|\not{\nabla}f|^{2}.

Proof.

See Propositions 4.2.2 and 4.2.3 in
[
5
]
.
∎

4.6
Comparison of
t
,
u
and
r

Lemma 4.21
.

We have the following estimates on the symmetry axis:

\displaystyle|t(p)-u(p)|\lesssim\varepsilon t(p),\qquad\forall p\in\varPhi.

(4.6)

In particular, we have

\displaystyle|u_{c}(t)-t|\lesssim\varepsilon t,\qquad\quad|t_{c}(u)-u|\lesssim\varepsilon t.

Proof.

Recalling that
\partial_{t}=\phi T
and

\displaystyle T(u)=1,\quad\mbox{ on }\varPhi,

we infer from (
4.1
)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}(t-u)|=|1-\phi T(u)|=|1-\phi|\lesssim\varepsilon,\quad\mbox{ on }\varPhi.

Integrating it along
\varPhi
, we deduce

\displaystyle|t(p)-u(p)|\leq|t(O)-u(O)|+\int_{0}^{t}|\partial_{t}(t-u)|dt\lesssim 0+\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon dt\lesssim\varepsilon t,\qquad\forall p\in\varPhi.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
4.21
.
∎

Lemma 4.22
.

We have the following estimates:

\displaystyle|r-(t-u)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon t.

Proof.

We have from Lemma
2.7

\displaystyle e_{4}(r)=\frac{\overline{\phi\tr\chi}}{2\phi}r.

Recalling that

\displaystyle\phi e_{4}(t)=\phi T(t)=1,\qquad\phi e_{4}(u)=0,

we infer from (
4.1
)

\displaystyle\left|\phi e_{4}(r-(t-u))\right|=\left|\frac{\overline{\phi\tr\chi}}{2}r-1\right|\lesssim\varepsilon.

Hence, we obtain in
{{}^{e}V}

\displaystyle|r(t,u)-(t-u)|\lesssim|r(0,u)+u|+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\phi e_{4}(r-(t-u))\right|dt\lesssim\varepsilon t,

where we used the fact that
|r+u|\leq\mathcal{O}_{(0)}\leq\varepsilon_{0}
on
\Sigma_{0}
.

Applying Lemma
4.21
, we have in
{{}^{i}V}

\displaystyle|r(t,u)-(t-u)|\lesssim|t_{c}(u)-u|+\int_{t_{c}(u)}^{t}\left|\phi e_{4}(r-(t-u))\right|dt\lesssim\varepsilon t.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
4.22
.
∎

We define

\displaystyle{{}^{i}_{e}V}_{t_{*}}:=\left\{p\in{{}^{i}V}_{t_{*}}\Big/r\geq\frac{t}{2}\right\},\qquad\quad{{}^{i}_{i}V}_{t_{*}}:=\left\{p\in{{}^{i}V}_{t_{*}}\Big/r\leq\frac{t}{2}\right\}.

(4.7)

Corollary 4.23
.

We have the following equivalences on the symmetry axis:

\displaystyle u_{c}(t)\simeq t,\qquad t_{c}(u)\simeq u,\quad\mbox{ on }\;\varPhi.

We also have the following comparison results:

\displaystyle t+{\langle u\rangle}\lesssim r\quad\mbox{ in }\;{{}^{e}V}_{t_{*}},\qquad 1\leq u,\langle r\rangle\lesssim t\quad\mbox{ in }\;{{}^{i}V}_{t_{*}}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle 1\leq u\lesssim t\simeq r,\quad\mbox{ in }\;{{}^{i}_{e}V}_{t_{*}},\qquad r\lesssim t\simeq u\quad\mbox{ in }\;{{}^{i}_{i}V}_{t_{*}}.

Proof.

It follows directly from Lemmas
4.21
and
4.22
, and from (
4.7
).
∎

4.7
Evolution lemma

We recall the following evolution lemma, which will be used in Sections
6
and
7
.

Lemma 4.24
.

Under the assumption (
4.1
), the following holds:

1.

Let
U,F
be
k
-covariant
S
-tangent tensor fields satisfying the outgoing evolution equation

\not{\nabla}_{4}U+\lambda_{0}\tr\chi\,U=F.

Denoting
\lambda_{1}:=2(\lambda_{0}-\frac{1}{p})
, we have for all
p\in(1,\infty)
:

\displaystyle|r^{\lambda_{1}}U|_{p,S}(t,u)

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{\lambda_{1}}U|_{p,S}(t_{c}(u),u)+\int_{t_{c}(u)}^{t}|r^{\lambda_{1}}F|_{p,S}(t^{\prime},u)dt^{\prime}\qquad\forall u\geq 1,

\displaystyle|r^{\lambda_{1}}U|_{p,S}(t,u)

\displaystyle\lesssim|r^{\lambda_{1}}U|_{p,S}(2,u)+\int_{{\langle u\rangle}}^{r}|r^{\lambda_{1}}F|_{p,S}(t^{\prime},u)dr^{\prime}\qquad\qquad\forall u\leq 1.

2.

Let
V,\underline{F}
be k-covariant and S-tangent tensor fields satisfying the normal evolution equation

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{N}V+\lambda_{0}\tr\theta\,V=\underline{F}.

Denoting
\lambda_{1}=2(\lambda_{0}-\frac{1}{p})
, we have for all
p\in(1,\infty)
:

\displaystyle|r^{\lambda_{1}}V|_{p,S}(t,u)

\displaystyle\lesssim\lim_{u\to-\infty}|r^{\lambda_{1}}V|_{p,S}(t,u)+\int_{-\infty}^{u}|r^{\lambda_{1}}\underline{F}|_{p,S}(t,u^{\prime})du^{\prime},

\displaystyle|r^{\lambda_{1}}V|_{p,S}(t,u)

\displaystyle\lesssim\lim_{u\to u_{c}(t)}|r^{\lambda_{1}}V|_{p,S}(t,u)+\int_{u}^{u_{c}(t)}|r^{\lambda_{1}}\underline{F}|_{p,S}(t,u^{\prime})du^{\prime}.

Proof.

See Lemma 13.1.1 in
[
5
]
or Lemma 7 in
[
1
]
.
∎

5
Curvature estimates (Theorem
3.8
)

In this section, we prove Theorem
3.8
by the
r^{p}
–weighted estimate method introduced in
[
6
]
and applied to Bianchi equations in
[
12
,
18
]
, see also
[
25
]
.

Throughout Section
5
, we denote

\displaystyle{{}^{e}V}

\displaystyle:=V_{t}(-\infty,u),\qquad\qquad\quad\,{{}^{i}V}:=V_{t}(u_{1},u_{2}),

\displaystyle{{}^{i}_{e}V}

\displaystyle:={{}^{i}V}\cap\left\{r\geq\frac{t}{2}\right\},\qquad\quad\;{{}^{i}_{i}V}:={{}^{i}V}\cap\left\{r\leq\frac{t}{2}\right\}.

5.1
Preliminaries

5.1.1
Schematic notation
\mathbb{E}_{q}
,
\mathbb{F}_{q}
and
\mathbb{O}_{\ell}

Definition 5.1
.

For a quantity
X
, we denote

X\in\mathbb{E}_{q}(s),

if
X
satisfies the following estimates:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{q,s-q}^{1}[X]+\mathcal{D}_{q,s-q}[X]+{{}^{i}\mathcal{E}}_{0,s}^{1}[X]+{{}^{i}\mathcal{D}}_{-2,s+2}[X]\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}.

Similarly, we denote

X\in\mathbb{F}_{q}(s),

if
X
satisfies the following estimates:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{q,s-q}^{1}[X]+\mathcal{D}_{q-1,s-q+1}[X]+{{}^{i}\mathcal{F}}_{0,s}^{1}[X]+{{}^{i}\mathcal{D}}_{-2,s+2}[X]\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}.

Moreover, we denote

\displaystyle X\in\mathbb{O}_{\ell}(s),

if
X
satisfies the following estimates:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{1}_{\ell-2,s-\ell}[X]+{{}^{i}\mathcal{D}}^{1}_{-3,s+1}[X]\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}.

We also denote

\displaystyle X\in\mathbb{E}_{q}^{(1)}(s)\qquad\,\mbox{ if }\quad\quad\qquad\;\;\;\mathcal{E}_{q,s-q}[X]+{{}^{i}\mathcal{E}}_{0,s}[X]

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2},

\displaystyle X\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{(1)}(s)\qquad\mbox{ if }\quad\,\quad\qquad\;\mathcal{F}_{q,s-q}[X]+{{}^{i}\mathcal{F}}_{0,s}[X]

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2},

\displaystyle X\in\mathbb{O}_{\ell}^{(1)}(s)\qquad\mbox{ if }\quad\quad\mathcal{D}_{\ell-2,s-\ell}[X]+{{}^{i}\mathcal{D}}_{-3,s+1}[X]

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}.

We also use the following shorthand notation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{X}_{q}:=\mathbb{X}_{q}(s),\qquad\forall\;\mathbb{X}\in\{\mathbb{E},\,\mathbb{F},\,\mathbb{O}\}.

Remark 5.2
.

Under the assumptions (
4.1
), we have

\displaystyle(\alpha,\beta,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma})

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{E}_{s}\cap\mathbb{F}_{s},\qquad\qquad\underline{\beta}\in\mathbb{E}_{0}\cap\mathbb{F}_{s},\qquad\qquad\underline{\alpha}\in\mathbb{F}_{0},

\displaystyle\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{E}_{s}^{(1)},\qquad\qquad\quad\underline{\alpha}_{3}\in\mathbb{F}_{-2}^{(1)}.

We also have
9
9

9

Recall that
{\delta_{B}}
is defined in (
3.4
).

\displaystyle(\rho,\sigma,{\not{U}})\in\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}},\qquad r^{-1}\Gamma_{i}^{(1)}\in\mathbb{F}_{s-{\delta_{B}}},\qquad i=g,b,w.

Moreover, we have from Lemma
4.2

\displaystyle\Gamma_{g}\in\mathbb{O}_{s},\qquad\Gamma_{b}\in\mathbb{O}_{1},\qquad\Gamma_{w}\in\mathbb{O}_{s-1}.

We also have from Corollary
4.11

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{T}\alpha

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{E}_{s+2}^{(1)}(s+2),\qquad\,\not{\nabla}_{T}\beta\in\mathbb{E}^{(1)}_{2s-{\delta_{B}}}(s+2),\qquad\,\not{\nabla}_{T}(\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{F}_{s+1}^{(1)}(s+2),

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{T}\underline{\beta}

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{(1)}(s+2),\qquad\;\;\not{\nabla}_{T}\underline{\alpha}\in\mathbb{F}_{0}^{(1)}(s+2).

5.1.2
Bianchi equations in schematic form

Proposition 5.3
.

We have the following Bianchi equations:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\not{\nabla}_{3}\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\alpha&=-2\not{d}_{2}^{*}\beta+\mathbb{O}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s},\\
\not{\nabla}_{4}\beta+2\tr\chi\,\beta&=\not{d}_{2}\alpha+\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s},\\
\not{\nabla}_{3}\beta+\tr\underline{\chi}\,\beta&=-\not{d}_{1}^{*}(\rho,-\sigma)+\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s}+\mathbb{O}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}},\\
\not{\nabla}_{4}(\rho,-\sigma)+\frac{3}{2}\tr\chi(\rho,-\sigma)&=\not{d}_{1}\beta+\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s},\\
\not{\nabla}_{3}(\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma})+\frac{3}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}(\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma})&=-\not{d}_{1}\underline{\beta}+\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s-{\delta_{B}}},\\
\not{\nabla}_{4}\underline{\beta}+\tr\chi\,\underline{\beta}&=\not{d}_{1}^{*}(\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma})+\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}}+\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s-{\delta_{B}}},\\
\not{\nabla}_{3}\underline{\beta}+2\tr\underline{\chi}\,\underline{\beta}&=-\not{d}_{2}\underline{\alpha}+\mathbb{O}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0},\\
\not{\nabla}_{4}\underline{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi\,\underline{\alpha}&=2\not{d}_{2}^{*}\underline{\beta}+\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0}.\end{split}

(5.1)

Proof.

It follows directly from Remark
5.2
, Lemma
4.9
and Proposition
4.10
.
∎

Commuting (
5.1
) with
\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}
, applying Propositions
2.15
and
4.5
and ignoring the terms that decay better, we obtain the following corollary.
10
10

10

The linear terms
r^{-1}O(\beta)
and
r^{-1}O(\underline{\beta})
come from the fact that
[\not{{\mathfrak{d}}},2\not{d}_{2}^{*}]=O(r^{-1})
, see Proposition
2.15
.

Corollary 5.4
.

We have the following equations:

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{3}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\alpha)+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\alpha)

\displaystyle=-\not{d}_{1}^{*}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta)+r^{-1}O(\beta)+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta)+2\tr\chi(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta)

\displaystyle=\not{d}_{1}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\alpha)+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{3}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta)+\tr\underline{\chi}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta)

\displaystyle=-\not{d}_{1}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\rho,-\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\sigma)+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s})^{(1)}+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\rho,-\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\sigma)+\frac{3}{2}\tr\chi(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\rho,-\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\sigma)

\displaystyle=\not{d}_{1}^{*}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta)+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{3}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\rho},\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\sigma})+\frac{3}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\rho},\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\sigma})

\displaystyle=-\not{d}_{1}^{*}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s-{\delta_{B}}})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})+\tr\chi(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})

\displaystyle=\not{d}_{1}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\rho},\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\sigma})+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}})^{(1)}+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s-{\delta_{B}}})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{3}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})+2\tr\underline{\chi}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})

\displaystyle=-\not{d}_{1}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\alpha})+(\mathbb{O}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\alpha})+\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\not{d}_{1}^{*}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})+r^{-1}O(\underline{\beta})+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}.

Lemma 5.5
.

We define the following quantities:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\alpha_{4}&:=\frac{1}{r^{4}}\not{\nabla}_{4}(r^{5}\alpha)\in\mathfrak{s}_{2},\qquad\quad\not{\alpha}:=r\not{d}_{2}\alpha\in\mathfrak{s}_{1},\\
\underline{\alpha}_{3}&:=\frac{1}{r^{4}}\not{\nabla}_{3}(r^{5}\underline{\alpha})\in\mathfrak{s}_{2},\qquad\quad\underline{\not{\alpha}}:=r\not{d}_{2}\underline{\alpha}\in\mathfrak{s}_{1}.\end{split}

(5.2)

Then, we have the following equations:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\not{\nabla}_{3}\alpha_{4}&=-2\not{d}_{2}^{*}\not{\alpha}+\frac{4\alpha}{r}+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)},\\
\not{\nabla}_{4}\not{\alpha}+\frac{5}{2}\tr\chi\,\not{\alpha}&=\not{d}_{2}{\alpha_{4}}+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)},\end{split}

(5.3)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\not{\nabla}_{4}\underline{\alpha}_{3}&=-2\not{d}_{2}^{*}\underline{\not{\alpha}}+\frac{4\underline{\alpha}}{r}+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)},\\
\not{\nabla}_{3}\underline{\not{\alpha}}+\frac{5}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\underline{\not{\alpha}}&=\not{d}_{2}\underline{\alpha}_{3}+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}.\end{split}

(5.4)

Proof.

See Lemma 2.26 in
[
25
]
.
∎

5.1.3
General Bianchi pairs

The following lemma provides the general structure of Bianchi pairs. It will be used repeatedly in Sections
5.2
–
5.4
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
k=1,2
and
a_{(1)}
,
a_{(2)}
real numbers. Then, we have the following properties.

1.

If
\psi_{(1)},h_{(1)}\in\mathfrak{s}_{k}
and
\psi_{(2)},h_{(2)}\in\mathfrak{s}_{k-1}
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}\not{\nabla}_{3}(\psi_{(1)})+a_{(1)}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\psi_{(1)}&=-k\not{d}_{k}^{*}(\psi_{(2)})+h_{(1)},\\
\not{\nabla}_{4}(\psi_{(2)})+a_{(2)}\tr\chi\,\psi_{(2)}&=\not{d}_{k}(\psi_{(1)})+h_{(2)}.\end{split}

(5.5)

Then, the pair
(\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)})
satisfies for any real number
p

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}e_{3})+k\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}e_{4})\\
+&\left(2a_{(1)}-1-\frac{p}{2}\right)r^{p}\tr\underline{\chi}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+k\left(2a_{(2)}-1-\frac{p}{2}\right)r^{p}\tr\chi|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\\
=&2kr^{p}{\not{\sdiv}\,}(\psi_{(1)}\cdot\psi_{(2)})+2r^{p}\psi_{(1)}\cdot h_{(1)}+2kr^{p}\psi_{(2)}\cdot h_{(2)}-2r^{p}\underline{\omega}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}\\
-&2kr^{p}\omega|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+pr^{p-1}\left(e_{3}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\right)|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+kpr^{p-1}\left(e_{4}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\chi\right)|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}.\end{split}

(5.6)

2.

If
\psi_{(1)},h_{(1)}\in\mathfrak{s}_{k-1}
and
\psi_{(2)},h_{(2)}\in\mathfrak{s}_{k}
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}\not{\nabla}_{3}(\psi_{(1)})+a_{(1)}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\psi_{(1)}&=\not{d}_{k}(\psi_{(2)})+h_{(1)},\\
\not{\nabla}_{4}(\psi_{(2)})+a_{(2)}\tr\chi\,\psi_{(2)}&=-k\not{d}_{k}^{*}(\psi_{(1)})+h_{(2)}.\end{split}

(5.7)

Then, the pair
(\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)})
satisfies for any real number
p

\displaystyle\begin{split}&k\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}e_{3})+\mathbf{Div}(r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}e_{4})\\
+&k\left(2a_{(1)}-1-\frac{p}{2}\right)r^{p}\tr\underline{\chi}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\left(2a_{(2)}-1-\frac{p}{2}\right)r^{p}\tr\chi|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\\
=&2r^{p}{\not{\sdiv}\,}(\psi_{(1)}\cdot\psi_{(2)})+2kr^{p}\psi_{(1)}\cdot h_{(1)}+2r^{p}\psi_{(2)}\cdot h_{(2)}-2kr^{p}\underline{\omega}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}\\
-&2r^{p}\omega|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+kpr^{p-1}\left(e_{3}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\right)|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+pr^{p-1}\left(e_{4}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\chi\right)|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}.\end{split}

(5.8)

Proof.

See Lemma 4.2 in
[
25
]
.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

Note that the Bianchi equations in (
5.1
), (
5.3
) and (
5.4
) can be written as systems of equations of the type (
5.5
) and (
5.7
). In particular

•

the Bianchi pair
(\alpha_{4},\not{\alpha})
satisfies (
5.5
) with
k=2
,
a_{(1)}=0
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{5}{2}
,

•

the Bianchi pair
(\alpha,\beta)
satisfies (
5.5
) with
k=2
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{1}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=2
,

•

the Bianchi pair
(\beta,(\rho,-\sigma))
satisfies (
5.5
) with
k=1
,
a_{(1)}=1
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{3}{2}
,

•

the Bianchi pair
((\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}),\underline{\beta})
satisfies (
5.7
) with
k=1
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{3}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=1
,

•

the Bianchi pair
(\underline{\beta},\underline{\alpha})
satisfies (
5.7
) with
k=2
,
a_{(1)}=2
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{1}{2}
,

•

the Bianchi pair
(\underline{\not{\alpha}},\underline{\alpha}_{3})
satisfies (
5.7
) with
k=2
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{5}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=0
.

5.1.4
Estimates for nonlinear error terms

The following theorem provides a unified treatment of all the nonlinear error terms in curvature estimates.

Theorem 5.8
.

We define
11
11

11

In order to simplify the notations,
V
can be ignored if it is clear in the context.

\mathcal{N}_{p}^{q}\left[\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)}\right](V):=\int_{V}r^{p}\left|\psi_{(1)}^{(q)}\cdot\psi_{(2)}^{(q)}\right|.

(5.9)

Let
p_{1},p_{2},p,\ell\leq s
and let
s>1
. Then, we have the following properties:

1.

In the case
2p<p_{1}+p_{2}+\ell+1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1}_{p}\left[\mathbb{E}_{p_{1}},\mathbb{O}_{\ell}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{p_{2}}\right]({{}^{e}V})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s-p}}.

2.

In the case
2p<p_{1}+p_{2}+\ell
and
2p+1<p_{1}+p_{2}+s
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1}_{p}\left[\mathbb{E}_{p_{1}},\mathbb{O}_{\ell}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{p_{2}}\right]({{}^{e}V})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s-p}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1}_{p}\left[\mathbb{F}_{p_{1}},\mathbb{O}_{\ell}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{p_{2}}\right]({{}^{e}V})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s-p}}.

3.

In the case
2p<p_{1}+p_{2}+\ell-1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1}_{p}\left[\mathbb{F}_{p_{1}},\mathbb{O}_{\ell}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{p_{2}}\right]({{}^{e}V})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s-p}}.

If in addition we assume that
p_{1},p_{2},p\geq 0
. Then, we have the following properties:

1.

In the case
2p<p_{1}+p_{2}+\ell+1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1}_{p}\left[\mathbb{E}_{p_{1}},\mathbb{O}_{\ell}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{p_{2}}\right]({{}^{i}V})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}.

2.

In the case
2p<p_{1}+p_{2}+\ell
and
2p+1<p_{1}+p_{2}+s
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1}_{p}\left[\mathbb{E}_{p_{1}},\mathbb{O}_{\ell}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{p_{2}}\right]({{}^{i}V})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1}_{p}\left[\mathbb{F}_{p_{1}},\mathbb{O}_{\ell}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{p_{2}}\right]({{}^{i}V})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}.

3.

In the case
2p<p_{1}+p_{2}+\ell-1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1}_{p}\left[\mathbb{F}_{p_{1}},\mathbb{O}_{\ell}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{p_{2}}\right]({{}^{i}V})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}.

Proof.

See Appendix
A
.
∎

5.1.5
Strategy of the proof of Theorem
3.8

The proof of Theorem
3.8
proceeds in 4 steps which we summarize below for convenience:

•

In Section
5.2
, we apply
r^{p}
–weighted estimates with
-2\leq p\leq s
to the Bianchi equations to control the curvature components in the exterior region
{{}^{e}V}
.

•

In Section
5.3
, we apply
r^{p}
–weighted estimates with
0\leq p\leq s
12
12

12

Notice that the restriction
p\geq 0
is necessary according to the fact that
r^{p}
is unbounded near
\varPhi
for
p<0
.
to the Bianchi equations to control the curvature components in the interior region
{{}^{i}V}={{}^{i}_{e}V}\cup{{}^{i}_{i}V}
. In
{{}^{i}_{e}V}
, the desired decay of curvature components follows directly from the fact that
{\langle u\rangle}\lesssim r
holds in
{{}^{i}_{e}V}
. On the other hand, in
{{}^{i}_{i}V}
, by the mean value method introduced in
[
6
]
, we can only deduce
r^{-\frac{3}{2}}u^{-\frac{s}{2}}
–decay for curvature components. The goal of the last two steps is then to recover the expected
r^{-\frac{1}{2}}u^{-\frac{s+2}{2}}
–decay for curvature components in
{{}^{i}_{i}V}
.

•

In Section
5.4
, we first commute the Bianchi equations with
\not{\nabla}_{T}
. We then apply
r^{p}
–weighted estimates with
0\leq p\leq s+2
to the commuted Bianchi equations to control
\not{\nabla}_{T}{\mathbf{R}}
. By the mean value method, we deduce
r^{-\frac{3}{2}}u^{-\frac{s+2}{2}}
–decay for
\not{\nabla}_{T}{\mathbf{R}}
in
{{}^{i}_{i}V}
.

•

Finally, in Section
5.5
, we apply the elliptic estimates of Proposition
4.19
to the Maxwell type systems in Proposition
2.23
to deduce the desired
r^{-\frac{1}{2}}u^{-\frac{s+2}{2}}
–decay for
{\mathbf{R}}
from the estimate of
\not{\nabla}_{T}{\mathbf{R}}
in
{{}^{i}_{i}V}
.

5.2
Exterior region estimates

Throughout Section
5.2
, we always assume that
u\leq 1
and we denote

V:=V_{t}(u)=V_{t}(-\infty,u).

Proposition 5.9
.

For
\psi_{(1)}
,
\psi_{(2)}
and
h_{(1)}
,
h_{(2)}
satisfying (
5.5
) or (
5.7
), we have the following properties:

•

In the case
2+p-4a_{(1)}>0
and
4a_{(2)}-2-p>0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&BEF_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u)+BF_{p}[\psi_{(2)}](u)\\
\lesssim&F_{p}[\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)}](\Sigma_{2}\cap V)+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}[\psi_{(1)},h_{(1)}]+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}[\psi_{(2)},h_{(2)}].\end{split}

(5.10)

•

In the case
2+p-4a_{(1)}\leq 0
and
4a_{(2)}-2-p>0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&EF_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u)+BF_{p}[\psi_{(2)}](u)\\
\lesssim&F_{p}[\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)}](\Sigma_{2}\cap V)+B_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u)+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}[\psi_{(1)},h_{(1)}]+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}[\psi_{(2)},h_{(2)}].\end{split}

(5.11)

•

In the case
2+p-4a_{(1)}\leq 0
and
4a_{(2)}-2-p\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&EF_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u)+F_{p}[\psi_{(2)}](u)\\
\lesssim&F_{p}[\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)}](\Sigma_{2}\cap V)+B_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u)+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}[\psi_{(1)},h_{(1)}]+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}[\psi_{(2)},h_{(2)}].\end{split}

(5.12)

Proof.

We have from Lemma
2.7

\displaystyle e_{4}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\chi=\frac{\overline{\phi\tr\chi}}{2\phi}r-\frac{r}{2}\tr\chi=\frac{r}{2\phi}(\overline{\phi\tr\chi}-\phi\tr\chi)\in r\Gamma_{g},

and similarly

\displaystyle e_{3}(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}=e_{4}(r)-2N(r)-\frac{r}{2}(\tr\chi-2\tr\theta)=r\Gamma_{g}-2\left(N(r)-\frac{r}{2}\tr\theta\right)\in r\Gamma_{b}.

Applying Lemma
4.2
, we infer

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\;\;\;\,\,\,\int_{V}r^{p}\left(|\Gamma_{b}||\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+|\Gamma_{g}||\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\right)\\
&\lesssim\int_{V}\frac{\varepsilon}{{\langle u\rangle}^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{V}\frac{\varepsilon}{t^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon\int_{-\infty}^{u}\left({\langle u\rangle}^{-\frac{s+1}{2}}\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}\right)du+\varepsilon\int_{2}^{t}\left(t^{-\frac{s+1}{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{t}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\right)dt\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon\sup_{u}\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}\right)\int_{-\infty}^{u}{\langle u\rangle}^{-\frac{s+1}{2}}du+\varepsilon\sup_{t}\left(\int_{\Sigma_{t}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\right)\int_{2}^{t}t^{-\frac{s+1}{2}}dt\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon\sup_{u}\left(\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}\right)+\varepsilon\sup_{t}\left(\int_{\Sigma_{t}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\right).\end{split}

(5.13)

Integrating (
5.6
) or (
5.8
) in
V
13
13

13

See Figure
2
for a geometric description of
V
.
, applying Stokes formula and reminding that
\tr\chi\simeq\frac{2}{r}
,
\tr\underline{\chi}\simeq-\frac{2}{r}
,
\omega,\underline{\omega}\in\Gamma_{g}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{\Sigma_{t}^{V}}r^{p}(|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+|\psi_{(2)}|^{2})+\int_{\mathcal{I}_{*}\cap V}r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{V}(2+p-4a_{(1)})r^{p-1}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+(4a_{(2)}-2-p)r^{p-1}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{2}\cap V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}||h_{(1)}|+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}||h_{(2)}|+r^{p}|\Gamma_{b}||\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\Gamma_{g}||\psi_{(2)}|^{2},

where
\mathcal{I}_{*}
denotes the future null infinity. Taking the supremum of
u
and
t
and applying (
5.13
), we obtain for
\varepsilon
small enough

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{u}\int_{{C_{u}^{V}}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\sup_{t}\int_{\Sigma_{t}^{V}}r^{p}(|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+|\psi_{(2)}|^{2})\\
+&\int_{V}(2+p-4a_{(1)})r^{p-1}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+(4a_{(2)}-2-p)r^{p-1}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}\\
\lesssim&\int_{\Sigma_{2}\cap V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}||h_{(1)}|+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}||h_{(2)}|,\end{split}

(5.14)

which implies

\displaystyle EF_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u)+F_{p}[\psi_{(2)}](u)+(2+p-4a_{(1)})B_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u)+(4a_{(2)}-2-p)B_{p}[\psi_{(2)}](u)

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle F_{p}[\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)}](\Sigma_{2}\cap V)+B_{p}[\psi_{(2)}](u)+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}[\psi_{(1)},h_{(1)}]+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}[\psi_{(2)},h_{(2)}].

Hence, (
5.10
)–(
5.12
) hold in the corresponding range of parameters. This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.9
.
∎

Domain of integration
V_{t}(u)

The following lemma allows us to obtain
{\langle u\rangle}
–decay of curvature along
\Sigma_{2}\cap V
.

Lemma 5.10
.

We have the following estimate for
p\leq s
and
u\leq 1
:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{2}\cap V}r^{p}\left(|\alpha^{(1)}|^{2}+|\beta^{(1)}|^{2}+|(\wideparen{\rho}^{(1)},\wideparen{\sigma}^{(1)})|^{2}+|\underline{\beta}^{(1)}|^{2}+|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s-p}}.

Proof.

It follows directly from the fact that
{\langle u\rangle}\lesssim r
on
\Sigma_{2}\cap V
.
∎

5.2.1
Estimates for the Bianchi pair
(\alpha,\beta)

Proposition 5.11
.

We have the following estimate:

BEF_{s}^{1}[\alpha](u)+BF_{s}^{1}[\beta](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

(5.15)

Proof.

We recall from Proposition
5.3

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{3}\alpha+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\alpha

\displaystyle=-2\not{d}_{2}^{*}\beta+\mathbb{O}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}\beta+2\tr\chi\,\beta

\displaystyle=\not{d}_{2}\alpha+\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}.

Applying (
5.10
) with
\psi_{(1)}=\alpha
,
\psi_{(2)}=\beta
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{1}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=2
,
h_{(1)}=\mathbb{O}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}
,
h_{(2)}=\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}
,
p=s
and noticing that

2+p-4a_{(1)}=s>0,\qquad 4a_{(2)}-2-p=6-s>0,

we obtain from (
5.9
) and Lemma
5.10

\displaystyle BEF_{s}[\alpha](u)+BF_{s}[\beta](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}].

Applying Theorem
5.8
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{0}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}]\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

Hence, we have

BEF_{s}[\alpha](u)+BF_{s}[\beta](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

(5.16)

Next, we recall from Corollary
5.4

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{3}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\alpha)+\frac{1}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\alpha)

\displaystyle=-\not{d}_{1}^{*}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta)+r^{-1}O(\beta)+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta)+2\tr\chi(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta)

\displaystyle=\not{d}_{1}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\alpha)+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)}.

Applying (
5.10
) with
\psi_{(1)}=\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\alpha
,
\psi_{(2)}=\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{1}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=2
,
h_{(1)}=r^{-1}O(\beta)+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)}
,
h_{(2)}=(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)}
and
p=s
, we infer

\displaystyle BEF_{s}^{1}[\alpha](u)+BF_{s}^{1}[\beta](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{s-1}^{0}[\alpha^{(1)},\beta]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}].

We have from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that for all
\delta_{0}>0

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s-1}^{0}[\alpha^{(1)},\beta]=\int_{V}r^{s-1}|\alpha^{(1)}||\beta|\leq\delta_{0}B_{s}^{1}[\alpha](u)+(4\delta_{0})^{-1}B_{s}[\beta](u).

Moreover, applying Theorem
5.8
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}]\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

Hence, combining with (
5.16
), we have for
\delta_{0}
small enough

BEF_{s}^{1}[\alpha](u)+BF_{s}^{1}[\beta](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

(5.17)

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.11
.
∎

5.2.2
Estimates for the Bianchi pair
(\beta,(\rho,-\sigma))

Proposition 5.12
.

We have the following estimate:

BEF_{s}^{1}[\beta](u)+BF_{s}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

(5.18)

Proof.

We recall from Corollary
5.4

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{3}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta)+\tr\underline{\chi}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta)

\displaystyle=-\not{d}_{1}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\rho,-\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\sigma)+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s})^{(1)}+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\rho,-\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\sigma)+\frac{3}{2}\tr\chi(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\rho,-\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\sigma)

\displaystyle=\not{d}_{1}^{*}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta)+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)}.

Applying (
5.11
) with
\psi_{(1)}=\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta
,
\psi_{(2)}=(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\rho,-\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\sigma)
,
a_{(1)}=1
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{3}{2}
,
h_{(1)}=(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s})^{(1)}+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)}
,
h_{(2)}=(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)}
and
p=s
, we obtain

\displaystyle BEF_{s}^{1}[\beta](u)+BF_{s}^{1}[\rho,\sigma](u)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\;F_{s}^{1}[\beta,\rho,\sigma](\Sigma_{2}\cap V)+B_{s}^{1}[\beta](u)+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\;\varepsilon_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}],

where we used (
5.17
) and Lemma
5.10
in the last step. Next, applying Theorem
5.8
in corresponding cases, we infer

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}]\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

Hence, we deduce

BEF_{s}^{1}[\beta](u)+BF_{s}^{1}[\rho,\sigma](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

(5.19)

Recalling (
3.3
), we infer

\displaystyle F_{s}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u)

\displaystyle\lesssim F_{s}^{1}[\rho,\sigma](u)+\int_{-\infty}^{u}r^{s+2}|r^{-\frac{2}{4}}\Gamma_{g}|^{2}_{4,S}|r^{-\frac{2}{4}}\Gamma_{w}|^{2}_{4,S}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}+\int_{-\infty}^{u}r^{s+2}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{r^{s+1}}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{r^{2}{\langle u\rangle}^{s-1}}du

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2},

and

\displaystyle B_{s}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u)

\displaystyle\lesssim B_{s}^{1}[\rho,\sigma](u)+\int_{2}^{t}dt\int_{-\infty}^{u}r^{s+1}|r^{-\frac{2}{4}}\Gamma_{g}|_{4,S}^{2}|r^{-\frac{2}{4}}\Gamma_{w}|^{2}_{4,S}du

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}+\int_{2}^{t}dt\int_{-\infty}^{u}r^{s+1}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{r^{s+1}}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{r^{2}{\langle u\rangle}^{s-1}}du

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.12
.
∎

5.2.3
Estimates for the Bianchi pair
((\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}),\underline{\beta})

Proposition 5.13
.

We have the following estimate:

BEF_{s}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u)+F_{s}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

(5.20)

Proof.

We recall from Corollary
5.4

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{3}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\rho},\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\sigma})+\frac{3}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\rho},\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\sigma})

\displaystyle=-\not{d}_{1}^{*}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s-{\delta_{B}}})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})+\tr\chi(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})

\displaystyle=\not{d}_{1}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\rho},\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\sigma})+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}})^{(1)}+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s-{\delta_{B}}})^{(1)}.

Applying (
5.12
) with
\psi_{(1)}=(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\rho},\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\sigma})
,
\psi_{(2)}=\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta}
,
a_{(1)}=1
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{3}{2}
,
h_{(1)}=(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)}
,
h_{(2)}=(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)}+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s})^{(1)}
and
p=s
, we obtain from (
5.18
) and Lemma
5.10

\displaystyle BEF_{s}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u)+F_{s}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\;\varepsilon_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s-{\delta_{B}}}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{F}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{F}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s-{\delta_{B}}}].

Applying Theorem
5.8
in corresponding cases, we deduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s-{\delta_{B}}}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{F}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}}]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{F}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s-{\delta_{B}}}]\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

(5.21)

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle BEF_{s}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u)+F_{s}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.13
.
∎

5.2.4
Estimates for the Bianchi pair
(\underline{\beta},\underline{\alpha})

Proposition 5.14
.

We have the following estimate:

BEF_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u)+F_{0}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s}}.

(5.22)

Proof.

We have from Corollary
5.4

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{3}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})+2\tr\underline{\chi}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})

\displaystyle=-\not{d}_{1}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\alpha})+(\mathbb{O}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\alpha})+\frac{1}{2}\tr\chi(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\not{d}_{1}^{*}(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})+r^{-1}O(\underline{\beta})+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}.

Applying (
5.12
) with
\psi_{(1)}=\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta}
,
\psi_{(2)}=\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\alpha}
,
a_{(1)}=2
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{1}{2}
,
h_{(1)}=(\mathbb{O}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}
,
h_{(2)}=(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}
and
p=0
, we obtain from Lemma
5.10

\displaystyle\begin{split}&EF_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u)+F_{0}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)\\
\lesssim&\;\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s}}+B_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u)+\mathcal{N}_{-1}^{0}[\underline{\alpha}^{(1)},\underline{\beta}]+\mathcal{N}_{0}^{1}[\mathbb{F}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0}]+\mathcal{N}^{1}_{0}[\mathbb{F}_{0},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0}].\end{split}

(5.23)

We first have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{-1}^{0}[\underline{\alpha}^{(1)},\underline{\beta}]

\displaystyle=\int_{2}^{t}\frac{dt}{(t+|u|)^{\frac{s+2}{2}}}\left(\int_{\Sigma_{t}^{V}}|\underline{\alpha}^{(1)}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Sigma_{t}^{V}}r^{s}|\underline{\beta}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{2}^{t}\frac{dt}{(t+|u|)^{\frac{s+2}{2}}}F_{0}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)^{\frac{1}{2}}F_{s}[\underline{\beta}](u)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{\frac{s}{2}}}\sup_{2\leq t\leq t_{*}}F_{0}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which implies that for all
\delta_{0}>0

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{-1}^{0}[\underline{\alpha}^{(1)},\underline{\beta}]\leq\delta_{0}\sup_{2\leq t\leq t_{*}}F_{0}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)+C\delta_{0}^{-1}\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s}},

(5.24)

where
C
is a positive constant independent of
\delta_{0}
. Next, applying (
5.12
) to the Bianchi pair
((\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\rho},\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\sigma}),\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta})
with
p=0
and proceeding as in Proposition
5.13
, we obtain from (
5.18
), Lemma
5.10
and Theorem
5.8

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u)&\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s}}+\mathcal{N}_{0}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{s-{\delta_{B}}}]+\mathcal{N}_{0}^{1}[\mathbb{F}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}}]+\mathcal{N}_{0}^{1}[\mathbb{F}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s-{\delta_{B}}}]\\
&\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s}}.\end{split}

(5.25)

Finally, applying Theorem
5.8
once again, we deduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{0}^{1}[\mathbb{F}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0}]+\mathcal{N}^{1}_{0}[\mathbb{F}_{0},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0}]\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s}}.

(5.26)

Injecting (
5.24
), (
5.25
) and (
5.26
) into (
5.23
), we have for
\delta_{0}
small enough

\displaystyle BEF_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u)+F_{0}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s}}.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.14
.
∎

5.2.5
Estimate for
\alpha_{4}

Proposition 5.15
.

We have the following estimate:

BEF_{s}[\alpha_{4}](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

(5.27)

Proof.

We have from (
5.3
)

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{3}\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle=-2\not{d}_{2}^{*}\not{\alpha}+\frac{4\alpha}{r}+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}\not{\alpha}+\frac{5}{2}\tr\chi\,\not{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\not{d}_{2}{\alpha_{4}}+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)}.

Applying (
5.10
) with
\psi_{(1)}=\alpha_{4}
,
\psi_{(2)}=\not{\alpha}
,
a_{(1)}=0
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{5}{2}
,
h_{(1)}=\frac{4\alpha}{r}+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)}
,
h_{(2)}=(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)}
and
p=s
, we deduce

\displaystyle BEF_{s}[\alpha_{4}](u)+BF_{s}^{1}[\alpha](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{s-1}^{0}[\alpha_{4},\alpha]+\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}].

Applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
5.17
), we obtain for any
\delta_{0}>0

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s-1}^{0}[\alpha_{4},\alpha]\leq\delta_{0}\int_{V}r^{s-1}|\alpha_{4}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4\delta_{0}}\int_{V}r^{s-1}|\alpha|^{2}\leq\delta_{0}B_{s}[\alpha_{4}](u)+(4\delta_{0})^{-1}B_{s}[\alpha].

Moreover, applying Theorem
5.8
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s}^{1}[\mathbb{E}_{s},\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s}]\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

Thus, we obtain for
\delta_{0}
small enough

\displaystyle BEF_{s}[\alpha_{4}](u)+BF_{s}^{1}[\alpha](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.15
.
∎

5.2.6
Estimate for
\underline{\alpha}_{3}

Proposition 5.16
.

We have the following estimate:

EF_{-2}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)+F_{-2}[\underline{\alpha}_{3}](u)\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s+2}}.

(5.28)

Proof.

We recall from (
5.4
)

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}\underline{\alpha}_{3}

\displaystyle=-2\not{d}_{2}^{*}\underline{\not{\alpha}}+\frac{4\underline{\alpha}}{r}+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{3}\underline{\not{\alpha}}+\frac{5}{2}\tr\underline{\chi}\,\underline{\not{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\not{d}_{2}\underline{\alpha}_{3}+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}.

Applying (
5.12
) with
\psi_{(1)}=\underline{\not{\alpha}}
,
\psi_{(2)}=\underline{\alpha}_{3}
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{5}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=0
,
h_{(1)}=(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}
,
h_{(2)}=\frac{4\underline{\alpha}}{r}+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}
and
p<-2
, we obtain from Lemma
5.10

\displaystyle EF_{p}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)+BF_{p}[\underline{\alpha}_{3}](u)\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s-p}}+B_{p}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)+\mathcal{N}_{p-1}^{0}[\underline{\alpha}_{3},\underline{\alpha}]+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}\left[\mathbb{F}_{-2}^{(1)},(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}\right].

Applying (
5.12
) to the Bianchi pair
(\underline{\beta},\underline{\alpha})
with
p<0
and proceeding as in Proposition
5.14
, we obtain from Lemma
5.10
and Theorem
5.8

\displaystyle B_{p}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s-p}},\qquad\forall p<0.

(5.29)

We have from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that for any
\delta_{0}>0
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{p-1}^{0}[\underline{\alpha}_{3},\underline{\alpha}]\leq\int_{V}r^{p-1}|\underline{\alpha}_{3}||\underline{\alpha}|\leq\delta_{0}B_{p}[\underline{\alpha}_{3}](u)+(4\delta_{0})^{-1}B_{p}[\underline{\alpha}](u).

We also have from Theorem
5.8

\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}\left[\mathbb{F}_{-2}^{(1)},(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}\right]\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{p}^{1}\left[\mathbb{F}_{-2},\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0}\right]\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s-p}}.

Combining the above estimates, we deduce for
\delta_{0}
small enough

\displaystyle B_{p}[\underline{\alpha}_{3}](u)\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s-p}},\qquad\forall p<-2.

(5.30)

Next, applying (
5.12
) with
\psi_{(1)}=\underline{\not{\alpha}}
,
\psi_{(2)}=\underline{\alpha}_{3}
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{5}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=0
,
h_{(1)}=(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}
,
h_{(2)}=\frac{4\underline{\alpha}}{r}+(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}
and
p=-2
, we obtain from Lemma
5.10
and (
5.29
)

\displaystyle EF_{-2}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)+F_{-2}[\underline{\alpha}_{3}](u)\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s+2}}+\mathcal{N}_{-3}^{0}[\underline{\alpha}_{3},\underline{\alpha}]+\mathcal{N}_{-2}^{0}\left[\mathbb{F}_{-2}^{(1)},(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}\right].

We then have from (
5.29
) and (
5.30
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{-3}^{0}[\underline{\alpha}_{3},\underline{\alpha}]\lesssim B_{-3}[\underline{\alpha}_{3}](u)^{\frac{1}{2}}B_{-1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s+2}}.

We also have from Theorem
5.8

\mathcal{N}_{-2}^{0}\left[\mathbb{F}_{-2}^{(1)},(\mathbb{O}_{s-1}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{0})^{(1)}\right]\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s+2}}.

Combining the above estimates, we deduce

\displaystyle EF_{-2}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u)+F_{-2}[\underline{\alpha}_{3}](u)\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{{\langle u\rangle}^{s+2}}.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.16
.
∎

5.3
Interior region estimates

Throughout Section
5.3
, we always assume that
1\leq u_{1}\leq u_{2}\leq u_{c}(t)
and we denote

\displaystyle V:=V_{t}(u_{1},u_{2}).

Also, we recall that

\displaystyle V={{}^{i}_{i}V}\cup{{}^{i}_{e}V},

where

{{}^{i}_{i}V}:=V\cap\left\{r\leq\frac{t}{2}\right\},\qquad\quad{{}^{i}_{e}V}:=V\cap\left\{r\geq\frac{t}{2}\right\}.

5.3.1
Domain of integration

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
\mathbf{C}_{\delta}
be the cylinder of radius
\delta>0
and centered on
\varPhi
, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{\delta}:=\{q\in\mathcal{M}\big/\,r(q)\leq\delta\}.

Then, for any
p\geq-1
, there exists a sequence
\delta_{n}\to 0
satisfying

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\partial\mathbf{C}_{\delta_{n}}\cap V}r^{p}|\Psi^{(1)}|^{2}=0,\qquad\Psi\in\{\alpha,\beta,\rho,\sigma,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma},\underline{\beta},\underline{\alpha}\},

where

\displaystyle\partial\mathbf{C}_{\delta}=\{q\in\mathcal{M}\big/\,r(q)=\delta\},

denotes the boundary of
\mathbf{C}_{\delta}
.

Proof.

We have from (
4.1
)

\displaystyle{{}^{i}\mathcal{F}}_{-2,s+2}^{1}[\Psi]\lesssim\varepsilon^{2},

which implies in particular

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}d\delta\int_{\partial\mathbf{C}_{\delta}\cap V}r^{-2}|\Psi^{(1)}|^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}t.

Noticing that
r=\delta
on
\partial\mathbf{C}_{\delta}
, we infer

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d\delta}{\delta^{p+2}}\int_{\partial\mathbf{C}_{\delta}\cap V}r^{p}|\Psi^{(1)}|^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}t<+\infty.

Hence, for
p\geq-1
, we deduce that there exists a sequence
\delta_{n}\to 0
satisfying

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\partial\mathbf{C}_{\delta_{n}}\cap V}r^{p}|\Psi^{(1)}|^{2}=0.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
5.17
.
∎

Figure 3:
Domain of integration
V_{t}(u_{1},u_{2})\setminus\mathbf{C}_{\delta}

We have the following analog of Proposition
5.9
.

Proposition 5.18
.

Let
p\geq-1
and let
\psi_{(1)}
and
\psi_{(2)}
be curvature components, i.e.

\displaystyle\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)}=R^{(q)},\quad\mbox{ where }\;R\in\{\alpha,\beta,(\rho,-\sigma),(\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}),\underline{\beta},\underline{\alpha}\}.

Assume that
\psi_{(1)}
and
\psi_{(2)}
satisfy (
5.5
) or (
5.7
). Then, we have

\displaystyle EF_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u_{2})+F_{p}[\psi_{(2)}](u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(2+p-4a_{(1)})B_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u_{1},u_{2})+(4a_{(2)}-2-p)B_{p}[\psi_{(2)}](u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\;E_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u_{1})+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}[\psi_{(1)},h_{(1)}]+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}[\psi_{(2)},h_{(2)}].

Proof.

Integrating (
5.6
) or (
5.8
) in
V\setminus\mathbf{C}_{\delta}
described in Figure
3
and proceeding as in Proposition
5.9
, we obtain for all
\delta>0

\displaystyle\int_{C_{u_{2}}^{V}\setminus\mathbf{C}_{\delta}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{{\Sigma_{t}^{V}}\setminus\mathbf{C}_{\delta}}r^{p}(|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+|\psi_{(2)}|^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{V}(2+p-4a_{(1)})r^{p-1}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+(4a_{(2)}-2-p)r^{p-1}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{C_{u_{1}}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{V\cap\partial\mathbf{C}_{\delta}(u_{1},u_{2})}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}||h_{(1)}|+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}||h_{(2)}|.

Taking
\delta=\delta_{n}
defined in Lemma
5.17
and letting
n\to\infty
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{C_{u}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{\Sigma_{t}^{V}}r^{p}(|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+|\psi_{(2)}|^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{V}(2+p-4a_{(1)})r^{p-1}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+(4a_{(2)}-2-p)r^{p-1}|\psi_{(2)}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{C_{u_{1}}^{V}}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{V}r^{p}|\psi_{(1)}||h_{(1)}|+r^{p}|\psi_{(2)}||h_{(2)}|.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.18
.
∎

5.3.2
Estimates for Bianchi pairs

The following lemmas allows us to obtain
u
–decay of curvature in the interior region.

Lemma 5.19
.

Let
s>1
,
q=0,1
and let
1\leq u_{1}\leq u_{2}\leq u_{c}(t)
. We assume that

\displaystyle pB_{p}^{q}[\Psi](u_{1},u_{2})+EF_{p}^{q}[\Psi](u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim E_{p}^{q}[\Psi](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u^{s-p}},\qquad\forall p\in[0,s],

(5.31)

and

E_{s}^{q}[\Psi](1)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle EF_{p}^{q}[\Psi](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad\forall p\in[0,s].

Proof.

We first have for all
u\geq 1

\displaystyle BEF_{s}^{q}[\Psi](1,u)\lesssim E_{s}^{q}[\Psi](1)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2},

which implies

E_{s}^{q}[\Psi](u)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2},\qquad\forall\;1\leq u\leq u_{c}(t).

(5.32)

Next, taking
u_{1}=2^{j}
and
u_{2}=2^{j+1}
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
in (
5.31
), we infer

B_{s}^{q}[\Psi](2^{j},2^{j+1})\lesssim E_{s}^{q}[\Psi](2^{j})\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle\int_{2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}}E_{s-1}^{q}[\Psi](u)du\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2},

which implies that there exists a sequence
u^{(j)}\in[2^{j},2^{j+1}]
such that

\displaystyle E_{s-1}^{q}[\Psi](u^{(j)})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u^{(j)}}.

Interpolating with (
5.32
), we obtain

\displaystyle E_{1}^{q}[\Psi](u^{(j)})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{\left(u^{(j)}\right)^{s-1}}.

Thus, applying (
5.31
) with
p=1
,
u_{1}=u^{(j-1)}
and
u_{2}=u\in[2^{j},2^{j+1}]
, we have

\displaystyle E_{1}^{q}[\Psi](u)\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{\left(u^{(j-1)}\right)^{s-1}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{(2^{j})^{s-1}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u^{s-1}}.

Next, we apply once again (
5.31
) with
p=1
to deduce

BEF_{1}^{q}[\Psi](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-1}}.

Then, we obtain for
j\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle B_{1}^{q}[\Psi](2^{j},2^{j+1})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{(2^{j})^{s-1}},

which implies

\displaystyle\int_{2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}}E_{0}^{q}[\Psi](u)du\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{(2^{j})^{s-1}}.

Then, there exists
v^{(j)}\in[2^{j},2^{j+1}]
such that

\displaystyle E_{0}^{q}[\Psi](v^{(j)})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{(2^{j})^{s}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{(v^{(j)})^{s}}.

Thus, applying (
5.31
) with
p=0
,
u_{1}=v^{(j-1)}
and
u_{2}=u\in[2^{j},2^{j+1}]
, we have

\displaystyle E_{0}^{q}[\Psi](u)\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{\left(v^{(j-1)}\right)^{s}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{(2^{j})^{s}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u^{s}}.

Next, we apply once again (
5.31
) with
p=0
to deduce

EF_{0}^{q}[\Psi](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}.

Interpolating with (
5.32
), this concludes the proof of Lemma
5.19
.
∎

Lemma 5.20
.

Let
s>1
,
q=0,1
and let
1\leq u_{1}\leq u_{2}\leq u_{c}(t)
. We assume that

\displaystyle BF_{p}^{q}[\Psi](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad p\in[0,1],

(5.33)

and

\displaystyle E_{0}^{q}[\Psi](u_{2})\lesssim E_{0}^{q}[\Psi](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}.

(5.34)

Then, we have

\displaystyle BEF_{0}^{q}[\Psi](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}.

(5.35)

Proof.

Applying (
5.33
) with
p=1
,
u_{1}=2^{j}
and
u_{2}=2^{j+1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}}E_{0}[\Psi](u)du\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{(2^{j})^{s-1}},

which implies that there exists a sequence
u^{(j)}\in[2^{j},2^{j+1}]
such that

\displaystyle E_{0}[\Psi](u^{(j)})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{(2^{j})^{s}}.

Next, applying (
5.34
) with
u_{1}=u^{(j-1)}
and
u_{2}=u\in[2^{j},2^{j+1}]
, we infer

\displaystyle E_{0}^{q}[\Psi](u)\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{(2^{j})^{s}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u^{s}}.

(5.36)

Finally, applying once again (
5.33
) with
p=0
and (
5.34
) and combining with (
5.36
), we obtain (
5.35
). This concludes the proof of Lemma
5.20
.
∎

Remark 5.21
.

The nonlinear error terms
\mathcal{N}_{p}^{q}[\psi_{(1)},h_{(1)}]
and
\mathcal{N}_{p}^{q}[\psi_{(2)},h_{(2)}]
in the proof of Propositions
5.22
and
5.24
below are the same as in Propositions
5.11
–
5.15
. Thus, we have from the second part of Theorem
5.8
14
14

14

We always have
p_{1},p_{2},p\geq 0
in the proof of Propositions
5.22
and
5.24
, so that the additional assumption of the second part of Theorem
5.8
is satisfied.

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{p}^{q}[\psi_{(1)},h_{(1)}]\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad\mathcal{N}_{p}^{q}[\psi_{(2)},h_{(2)}]\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}.

In the proof of Propositions
5.22
and
5.24
below, we thus focus on the linear terms.

Proposition 5.22
.

We have the following estimates for all
0\leq p\leq s
:

\displaystyle EF_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1},u_{2})+pB_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1},u_{2})+BF_{p}^{1}[\beta](u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},

\displaystyle BEF_{p}^{1}[\beta](u_{1},u_{2})+BF_{p}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\alpha,\beta](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},

\displaystyle BEF^{1}_{p}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1},u_{2})+(2-p)B_{p}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})+F^{1}_{p}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\alpha,\beta,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},

\displaystyle BEF_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})+F_{0}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim E_{0}^{1}[\alpha,\beta,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma},\underline{\beta}](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}.

Proof.

The proof is largely analogous to Propositions
5.11
–
5.15
. So we only provide a sketch. Throughout the proof, we always assume that
0\leq p\leq s
.

Applying Proposition
5.18
with
\psi_{(1)}=\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}^{\leq 1}\alpha
,
\psi_{(2)}=\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}^{\leq 1}\beta
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{1}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=2
, we obtain

\displaystyle EF^{1}_{p}[\alpha](u_{1},u_{2})+pB_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1},u_{2})+BF^{1}_{p}[\beta](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}.

(5.37)

Next, applying Proposition
5.18
with
\psi_{(1)}=\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\beta
,
\psi_{(2)}=(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\rho,-\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\sigma)
,
a_{(1)}=1
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{3}{2}
, we deduce

\displaystyle EF^{1}_{p}[\beta](u_{1},u_{2})+BF^{1}_{p}[\rho,\sigma](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\beta](u_{1})+B_{p}^{1}[\beta](u_{1},u_{2})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}.

(5.38)

Combining with (
5.37
) and (
3.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle BEF^{1}_{p}[\beta](u_{1},u_{2})+BF^{1}_{p}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\alpha,\beta](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}.

(5.39)

Next, applying Proposition
5.18
with
\psi_{(1)}=(\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\rho},\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\wideparen{\sigma})
,
\psi_{(2)}=\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}\underline{\beta}
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{3}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=1
, we infer

\displaystyle\begin{split}&EF^{1}_{p}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1},u_{2})+(2-p)B_{p}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})+F^{1}_{p}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})\\
\lesssim&\;E_{p}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1})+B_{p}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1},u_{2})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}.\end{split}

(5.40)

Combining with (
5.39
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&BEF^{1}_{p}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1},u_{2})+(2-p)B_{p}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})+F^{1}_{p}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})\\
\lesssim&\;E_{p}^{1}[\alpha,\beta,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}.\end{split}

(5.41)

Finally, applying Proposition
5.18
with
\psi_{(1)}=\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}^{\leq 1}\underline{\beta}
,
\psi_{(2)}=\not{{\mathfrak{d}}}^{\leq 1}\underline{\alpha}
,
a_{(1)}=2
,
a_{(2)}=\frac{1}{2}
and
p=0
, we deduce

\displaystyle EF_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})+F_{0}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1})+B_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}.

Combining with (
5.41
) in the case
p=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle BEF_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})+F_{0}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{0}^{1}[\alpha,\beta,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma},\underline{\beta}](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.22
.
∎

Proposition 5.23
.

We have the following estimates for all
0\leq p\leq s
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}pB_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1},u_{2})+B_{p}^{1}[\beta,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma},\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})+EF_{p}^{1}[\alpha,\beta,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1},u_{2})+F^{1}_{p}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})&\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\\
E_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})+F_{0}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u_{1},u_{2})&\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}.\end{split}

(5.42)

Proof.

Recall that we have from Propositions
5.11
–
5.15

\displaystyle E_{s}^{1}[\alpha,\beta,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](1)+E_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](1)\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

(5.43)

Applying Proposition
5.22
in the case
p=s
, we obtain immediately the first estimate of (
5.42
) in the case
p=s
. We then focus on the case
p=0
.

We have from Proposition
5.22

\displaystyle pB_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1},u_{2})+EF_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}.

(5.44)

Applying Lemma
5.19
with
\Psi=\alpha
, we obtain

\displaystyle EF_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad\forall p\in[0,s].

(5.45)

Next, we have from Proposition
5.22
and (
5.45
)

\displaystyle BF_{p}^{1}[\beta](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad\forall\;p\in[0,s],

and

\displaystyle E_{p}^{1}[\beta](u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\alpha,\beta](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\beta](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad\forall\;p\in[0,s].

Applying Lemma
5.20
with
\Psi=\beta
, we obtain

\displaystyle BEF_{0}^{1}[\beta](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}.

We also have from Proposition
5.22
and (
5.43
)

\displaystyle BEF_{s}^{1}[\beta](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{s}^{1}[\alpha,\beta](u_{1})+\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\lesssim E_{s}^{1}[\alpha,\beta](1)+\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

Combining the above estimates, we infer

\displaystyle BEF_{p}^{1}[\beta](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad\forall p\in[0,s].

(5.46)

Then, we have from Proposition
5.22
, (
5.45
) and (
5.46
)

\displaystyle BF_{p}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\alpha,\beta](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad\forall\;p\in[0,s],

and

\displaystyle E_{p}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\alpha,\beta,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad\forall\;p\in[0,s].

Applying Lemma
5.20
, we obtain

\displaystyle BEF_{0}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}.

We also have from Proposition
5.22
and (
5.43
)

\displaystyle BEF_{s}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}.

Combining the above estimates, we deduce

\displaystyle BEF_{p}^{1}[\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad\forall p\in[0,s].

(5.47)

Next, we have from Proposition
5.22
and (
5.45
)–(
5.47
)

\displaystyle BF_{p}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{p}^{1}[\alpha,\beta,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma}](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad p\in[0,s],

and

\displaystyle E_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{0}^{1}[\alpha,\beta,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma},\underline{\beta}](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}\lesssim E_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}.

Applying Lemma
5.20
, we deduce

\displaystyle BEF_{0}^{1}[\underline{\beta}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}.

(5.48)

Finally, we have from Proposition
5.22
and (
5.45
)–(
5.48
)

\displaystyle F_{0}^{1}[\underline{\alpha}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{0}^{1}[\alpha,\beta,\wideparen{\rho},\wideparen{\sigma},\underline{\beta}](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s}}.

(5.49)

Combining the above estimates, this concludes the proof of Proposition
5.23
.
∎

Proposition 5.24
.

We have the following estimate:

EF_{p}[\alpha_{4}](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad\forall\;p\in[0,s].

Proof.

We have from (
5.3
)

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{3}\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle=-2\not{d}_{2}^{*}\not{\alpha}+\frac{4\alpha}{r}+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)},

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{4}\not{\alpha}+\frac{5}{2}\tr\chi\,\not{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\not{d}_{2}{\alpha_{4}}+(\mathbb{O}_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s})^{(1)}.

Applying Proposition
5.18
with
\psi_{(1)}=\alpha_{4}
,
\psi_{(2)}=\not{\alpha}
,
a_{(1)}=\frac{1}{2}
,
a_{(2)}=2
and
0\leq p\leq s
, we deduce from Remark
5.21

\displaystyle BEF_{p}[\alpha_{4}](u_{1},u_{2})+BF_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{p}[\alpha_{4}](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}}+\mathcal{N}_{p-1}^{0}[\alpha_{4},\alpha].

Applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain for any
\delta_{0}>0

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{p-1}^{0}[\alpha_{4},\alpha]\leq\delta_{0}\int_{V}r^{p-1}|\alpha_{4}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4\delta_{0}}\int_{V}r^{p-1}|\alpha|^{2}\leq\delta_{0}B_{p}[\alpha_{4}](u_{1},u_{2})+(4\delta_{0})^{-1}B_{p}[\alpha](u_{1},u_{2}).

Combining with Proposition
5.22
, we obtain for
\delta_{0}
small enough,

\displaystyle BEF_{p}[\alpha_{4}](u_{1},u_{2})+BF_{p}^{1}[\alpha](u_{1},u_{2})\lesssim E_{p}[\alpha_{4}](u_{1})+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u_{1}^{s-p}},\qquad\forall\;p\in[0,s].

Applying Lemma
5.19
, we deduce

\displaystyle EF_{p}[\alpha_{4}](u)\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{u^{s-p}},\qquad\forall\;p\in[0,s].

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.24
.
∎

5.4
Time derivative estimates in the interior region

5.4.1
Bianchi equations commuted with
\not{\nabla}_{T}

Lemma 5.25
.

We have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\not{\nabla}_{T}\widehat{\chi}&=r^{-1}\mathbb{O}_{1}^{(1)},\qquad\quad\,\not{\nabla}_{T}\zeta=r^{-1}\mathbb{O}_{s-1}^{(1)},\qquad\quad\,\not{\nabla}_{T}\widehat{\underline{\chi}}=r^{-1}\mathbb{O}_{-1}^{(1)},\\
\not{\nabla}_{T}\tr\chi&=r^{-1}\mathbb{O}_{1}^{(1)},\qquad\not{\nabla}_{T}\tr\underline{\chi}=r^{-1}\mathbb{O}_{s-1}^{(1)},\end{split}

(5.50)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\not{\nabla}_{T}\eta&\in r^{-1}\mathbb{O}_{-1}^{(1)},\qquad\,\,\not{\nabla}_{T}\underline{\omega}\in r^{-1}\mathbb{O}_{s-1}^{(1)},\qquad\,\,\not{\nabla}_{T}\underline{\xi}\in r^{-1}\mathbb{O}_{-1}^{(1)},\\
\not{\nabla}_{T}\underline{\eta}&\in r^{-1}\mathbb{O}_{s-1}^{(1)},\qquad\not{\nabla}_{T}\omega\in r^{-1}\mathbb{O}_{s-1}^{(1)}.\end{split}

(5.51)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\not{\nabla}_{T}\alpha&\in\mathbb{E}_{s+2}^{(1)}(s+2),\qquad\,\not{\nabla}_{T}\beta\in\mathbb{E}^{(1)}_{2s-{\delta_{B}}}(s+2),\\
\not{\nabla}_{T}(\rho,\sigma)&\in\mathbb{F}^{(1)}_{s+1}(s+2),\qquad\;\not{\nabla}_{T}\underline{\beta}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{(1)}(s+2),\qquad\;\not{\nabla}_{T}\underline{\alpha}\in\mathbb{F}_{0}^{(1)}(s+2),\end{split}

(5.52)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}r^{-1}\alpha&\in\mathbb{E}_{s+2}(s+2),\qquad\;r^{-1}\beta\in\mathbb{E}_{s+2}(s+2),\qquad\;r^{-1}(\rho,\sigma)\in\mathbb{E}_{s+2}(s+2),\\
r^{-1}\underline{\beta}&\in\mathbb{F}_{s+2}(s+2),\qquad\,\,r^{-1}\underline{\alpha}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}(s+2).\end{split}

(5.53)

Proof.

It follows directly from (
2.14
), (
4.1
) and Corollaries
4.8
and
4.11
.
∎

Theorem 5.27
.

Let
0\leq p_{1},p_{2},p\leq s+2
,
\ell\leq s
and let
s>1
. Then, we have the following properties.

1.

In the case
2p<p_{1}+p_{2}+\ell+1
, we have

\mathcal{N}^{1}_{p}\left[\mathbb{E}_{p_{1}}(s+2),\mathbb{O}_{\ell}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{p_{2}}(s+2)\right]\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{u_{1}^{s+2-p}}.

2.

In the case
2p<p_{1}+p_{2}+\ell
and
2p+1<p_{1}+p_{2}+s
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1}_{p}\left[\mathbb{E}_{p_{1}}(s+2),\mathbb{O}_{\ell}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{p_{2}}(s+2)\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{u_{1}^{s+2-p}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1}_{p}\left[\mathbb{F}_{p_{1}}(s+2),\mathbb{O}_{\ell}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{p_{2}}(s+2)\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{u_{1}^{s+2-p}}.

3.

In the case
2p<p_{1}+p_{2}+\ell-1
, we have

\mathcal{N}^{1}_{p}\left[\mathbb{F}_{p_{1}}(s+2),\mathbb{O}_{\ell}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{p_{2}}(s+2)\right]\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{3}}{u_{1}^{s+2-p}}.

Proof.

The proof is largely analogous to Theorem
5.8
and left to the reader.
∎

Proposition 5.28
.

Let
\psi_{(1)}
and
\psi_{(2)}
be time derivative of curvature components, i.e.

\displaystyle\psi_{(1)},\psi_{(2)}\in\{\not{\nabla}_{T}\alpha,\not{\nabla}_{T}\beta,\not{\nabla}_{T}\rho,\not{\nabla}_{T}\sigma,\not{\nabla}_{T}\underline{\beta},\not{\nabla}_{T}\underline{\alpha}\}.

We also assume that
\psi_{(1)}
and
\psi_{(2)}
satisfy (
5.5
) or (
5.7
). Then, we have for
p\geq 0

\displaystyle EF_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u_{2})+F_{p}[\psi_{(2)}](u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(2+p-4a_{(1)})B_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u_{1},u_{2})+(4a_{(2)}-2-p)B_{p}[\psi_{(2)}](u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\;E_{p}[\psi_{(1)}](u_{1})+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}[\psi_{(1)},h_{(1)}]+\mathcal{N}_{p}^{0}[\psi_{(2)},h_{(2)}].

Proof.

Applying Theorem 7.1 in
[
24
]
, we construct a sequence of initial data
(\Sigma_{0},g_{n},k_{n})
which approximate
(\Sigma_{0},g,k)
in
H^{6}(\Sigma_{0})
. By local existence and Sobolev inequality, we obtain a sequence of
C^{4}
–solutions
(\mathcal{M}_{n},\mathbf{g}_{n})
of Einstein vacuum equations with initial data
(\Sigma_{0},g_{n},k_{n})
which approximate
(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{g})
. Denoting
(\mathbf{D}_{n},{\mathbf{R}}_{n})
the Levi-Civita connection and curvature components of
\mathbf{g}_{n}
, we have near the symmetry axis
\varPhi

\displaystyle E_{n},H_{n}=O(1),\qquad(\mathbf{D}_{n})_{T_{n}}(E_{n},H_{n})=O(1).

Recalling that
(e_{4})_{n}=T_{n}+N_{n}
,
(e_{3})_{n}=T_{n}-N_{n}
and
\|T_{n}\|,\|N_{n}\|=1
, we deduce on
\partial\mathbf{C}_{\delta}

\displaystyle(\not{\nabla}_{n})_{T_{n}}R_{n}=O(1),\qquad\mbox{ where }\;R\in\{\alpha,\beta,\rho,\sigma,\underline{\beta},\underline{\alpha}\}.

Integrating (
5.6
) or (
5.8
) in
V\setminus\mathbf{C}_{\delta}
16
16

16

Recall that
\mathbf{C}_{\delta}
is defined in Lemma
5.17
.
and proceeding as in Proposition
5.18
, we obtain
17
17

17

Note that in (
5.54
)–(
5.56
), the constants involved in
\lesssim
are independent of
n
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{C_{u_{2}}^{V}\setminus\mathbf{C}_{\delta}}r^{p}|(\psi_{n})_{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{{\Sigma_{t}^{V}}\setminus\mathbf{C}_{\delta}}r^{p}(|(\psi_{n})_{(1)}|^{2}+|(\psi_{n})_{(2)}|^{2})\\
+&\int_{V}(2+p-4a_{(1)})r^{p-1}|(\psi_{n})_{(1)}|^{2}+(4a_{(2)}-2-p)r^{p-1}|(\psi_{n})_{(2)}|^{2}\\
\lesssim&\int_{C_{u_{1}}^{V}}r^{p}|(\psi_{n})_{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{V\cap\partial\mathbf{C}_{\delta}(u_{1},u_{2})}|(\psi_{n})_{(1)}|^{2}+|(\psi_{n})_{(2)}|^{2}\\
+&\int_{V}r^{p}|(\psi_{n})_{(1)}||(h_{n})_{(1)}|+r^{p}|(\psi_{n})_{(2)}||(h_{n})_{(2)}|,\end{split}

(5.54)

where we used
r^{p}\leq 1
on

. Note that we have

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\int_{V\cap\partial\mathbf{C}_{\delta}(u_{1},u_{2})}|(\psi_{n})_{(1)}|^{2}+|(\psi_{n})_{(2)}|^{2}\lesssim C(n)t\lim_{\delta\to 0}\delta^{2}=0,

(5.55)

C(n)

. Taking
\delta\to 0

5.54

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{C_{u_{2}}^{V}}r^{p}|(\psi_{n})_{(1)}|^{2}+\int_{{\Sigma_{t}^{V}}}r^{p}(|(\psi_{n})_{(1)}|^{2}+|(\psi_{n})_{(2)}|^{2})\\

(5.56)

), this concludes the proof of Proposition
